115年 9月全國賦稅收入初步統計 
一、全國賦稅收入 
（一）本月數 
9 月實徵淨額 4,965 億元，較上年同月增加 813 億元(+19.6%)。 
（二）累計數 
累計 1 至 9 月實徵淨額 3 兆 5,714 億元，較上年同期增加 7,004 億
元(+24.4%)。 
（三）達成率 
1.中央政府總預算業於 9 月 18 日公布，稅課收入較原預算案提高
6,000 億元，併計地方政府及特種基金後，全國稅課收入較預算案
提高 5,852 億元，本次發布均改依法定數計算預算達成率(實徵淨
額占全年預算數)。 
2.累計 1 至 9 月實徵淨額占累計分配預算數 115.0%，占全年預算數
78.8%。 
圖 1 全國賦稅收入及年增率

說明：115 年 5-7 月年增率跳動較大，係因 114 年所得稅結算申報及繳納期限延長至 6 月底所致。

二、主要稅目別變動
（一）9 月實徵淨額與上年同月比較，以營業稅增加 297 億元、營利事業
所得稅增加 254 億元及證券交易稅增加 222 億元較多。
（二）累計 1 至 9 月實徵淨額與上年同期比較，以證券交易稅增加 3,077
億元、營利事業所得稅增加 1,853 億元及營業稅增加 1,203 億元較多。
圖 2 主要稅目別變化
(115 年 1-9 月)
1.營利事業所得稅
(1)9 月實徵淨額 2,007 億元，較上年同月增加 254 億元(+14.5%)，主
因暫繳稅款成長。
(2)累計 1 至 9 月實徵淨額 1 兆 28 億元，較上年同期增加 1,853 億元
(+22.7%)，主因結算申報自繳 、暫繳稅款 俱增及延分期稅款陸續
入帳。
2.綜合所得稅
9 月實徵淨額 490 億元，較上年同月增加 34 億元(+7.4%)，主因扣繳
稅款增加所致；累計 1 至 9 月實徵淨額 8,279 億元，較上年同期增加
790 億元(+10.6%)，主因結算申報自繳增加，以及盈餘分配、薪資所

得等扣繳稅款均呈成長。
3.營業稅
9月實徵淨額1,414億元，較上年同月增加297億元(+26.6%)，累計1至
9月實徵淨額6,230億元，較上年同期增加1,203億元(+23.9%)，均因內
需穩定，加以電子零組件 、資通與視聽產品 等進口代徵稅額增加 ，
惟出口暢旺，適用外銷零稅率營業人申請退稅亦增，抵銷部分增幅。

4.貨物稅
(1)9月實徵淨額100億元，較上年同月減少11億元(-10.0%)，主因汽柴
油減徵幅度擴大，惟電動車大量到港推升進口 車稅額，抵銷部分
減幅。
(2)累計1至9月實徵淨額874億元，較上年同期減少207億元(-19.2%)，
除汽柴油降稅因素外 ，亦與新購汽機車及電動車之減免徵退稅增
加有關。
5.證券交易稅
(1)9 月及累計 1 至 9 月上市上櫃股票平均每日成交值為 1 兆 317 億
元、1 兆 1,461 億元，分別較上年同期(6,006 億元、4,451 億元)增
71.8%、1.6 倍。
(2)9 月 證 交 稅 實 徵 淨 額534 億 元， 較 上 年 同 月 增 加 222 億 元
(+71.2%)；累計 1 至 9 月實徵淨額 5,060 億元，較上年同期增加
3,077 億元(+1.6 倍)。
6.關稅
9 月實徵淨額 157 億元，較上年同月增加 3 億元(+1.9%)，累計 1 至 9
月實徵淨額 1,224 億元，較上年同期增加 80 億元(+7.0%)，均因機械

及電機設備、基本金屬及其製品等進口稅額增加。
7.贈與稅
9 月實徵淨額 23 億元，較上年同月增加 6 億元(+35.7%)，累計 1 至 9
月實徵淨額 211 億元，較上年同期增加 43 億元(+25.3%)，主因大額
稅款案件增加。
8.土地增值稅
9 月實徵淨額 56 億元，較上年同月增加 12 億元(+26.6%)，主因大額
稅款案件增加；累計 1 至 9 月實徵淨額 504 億元，較上年同期減少
14 億元(-2.8%)，主因房地交易量及大額稅款案件減少。
9.印花稅
9 月實徵淨額 31 億元，較上年同月增加 4 億元(+16.2%)，累計 1 至 9
月實徵淨額 175 億元，較上年同期增加 15 億元(+9.0%)，主因銀行放
款稅額成長。
（三）前 9月主要稅目實徵淨額占分配預算數之比率：
1.大於 100%者有證交稅(272%)、遺產稅(155%)、贈與稅(132%)、印花
稅(130%)、營業稅(116%)、關稅(114%)、契稅(111%)、房屋稅(106%)、

綜所稅(101%)、牌照稅(101%)及營所稅(101%)等。
2.低於 100%者有貨物稅(98%)、菸酒稅(97%)、土增稅(85%)、地價稅
(84%)及特銷稅(57%)等。

三、各級政府賦稅收入概況
(一)中央政府
累計 1 至 9 月中央政府賦稅收入 (不含中央統籌分配稅款 )實徵淨 額
2 兆 3,603 億元(占分配預算數 119.3%)，較上年同期增加 2,553 億元
(+12.1%)，雖新版財政收支劃分法 開始施行，營業稅劃歸中央政府 比
率大幅調降，減少 2,748 億元(-90.7%)，惟證交稅、所得稅分別增加 3,077
億元、2,230 億元，互抵後呈現上升。
(二)地方政府
累計 1 至 9 月地方政府賦稅收
入(含中央統籌分配稅款 )實徵
淨額 1 兆 898 億元(占分配預
算數 109.4%)，較上年同期增
加 4,527 億元(+71.0%)，主因
納入中央統籌分配 之營業稅
增加 4,030 億元(+2.1 倍)所致。
(三)中央特種基金
累計 1 至 9 月撥入中央特種基金之健康福利捐實徵淨額 195 億元，較
上年同期增加 2 億元(+0.8%)；房地合一課徵所得稅撥入住宅基金 135
億元，較上年同期減少 7 億元(-4.7%)；撥入長照基金 883 億元(其中 541

億元來自房地合一課徵所得稅、195 億元來自菸稅，146 億元來自遺贈
稅)，較上年同期增加 8 億元(+0.9%)。

Document 065: 2026年10月通胀数据点评：菜价反季节下行压制食品价格，PPI同比涨幅或逐步见顶

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026年10月通胀数据点评：菜价反季节下行压制食品价格，PPI同比涨幅或逐步见顶.pdf
Report source: 西南证券
Date: 2026-11-10
Pages: 7
Status: ok

### Page 1

请务必阅读正文后的重要声明部分  
1 
[Table_ReportInfo] 2026 年 11 月 09 日      
证 券研究报告 •宏观简评报告      
数据点评  
菜价反季节下行压制食品价格，PPI 同比涨幅或逐步见顶 
——2026 年 10 月通胀数据点评

点评

西 南证券研究院  
[Table_Summary]  摘要：CPI方面，10 月 CPI 同比上涨 0.7%，涨幅较上月收窄0.2 个百分点，
低于市场预期，主要受食品价格拖累。 食品价格同比下降2.9%，降幅扩大 1.2
个百分点， 主要受气候条件适宜、 蔬菜供应充足影响， 鲜菜价格反季节性下行；
非食品价格同比上涨1.5%，涨幅与上月持平， 能源价格及AI 产业景气支撑消
费品价格，其中汽油价格同比上涨22.4%，数据存储设备、平板电脑等AI 相
关硬件价格保持较快上涨。环比看，CPI环比持平，食品价格由涨转跌，核心
CPI 同比上涨 0.8%，较上月回落 0.2 个百分点，反映终端需求修复仍偏温和。
展望 11 月，秋菜集中上市、南北供给错峰收窄，叠加猪肉供给充足，食品价
格或延续下行； 国际油价回落及节后出行需求季节性减弱， 也将削弱非食品价
格的支撑效应，CPI 同比或继续处于低位。PPI方面，10 月 PPI 同比上涨 5.7%，
涨幅较上月扩大 0.7个百分点，主要受能源价格上涨及基数效应推动。分行业
看，煤炭、石油开采及燃料加工业价格同比涨幅分别扩大至36.6%、35.0%和
29.9%， 化工、 化纤等行业价格亦明显回升， 能源涨价向中游原材料环节传导；
计算机、通信和其他电子设备制造业价格同比上涨5.9%，反映 AI 产业景气对
部分中游制造业价格形成支撑。 环比看，PPI上涨 0.7%，涨幅较上月收窄0.5
个百分点，能源行业仍是主要拉动，但涨价动能有所减弱。10 月 CPI 与 PPI
同比涨幅差扩大至 5 个百分点，利润进一步向上游集中，中下游企业或面临成
本上升与提价受限的双重压力。展望11 月，国际油价回落及翘尾因素下行或
推动 PPI 同比涨幅逐步见顶， 环比可能转为负增长或接近持平；然而冬季储煤
需求及安监政策或对煤价形成支撑，PPI 走势仍将呈现结构性分化。 
 食品拖累 CPI同比边际走弱，能源与 AI景气支撑非食品价格。同比来看，10
月 CPI同比涨幅收窄 0.2 个百分点至 0.7%， 增速低于wind一致预期 （0.83%），
主要受食品项拖累。其中，食品价格同比下降2.9%，降幅扩大 1.2 个百分点，
主要因气候条件适宜、蔬菜供给充足，带动菜价反季节下行。非食品价格同比
上升 1.5%，涨幅与上月持平，保持温和上涨态势；其中能源价格与AI 产业景
气支撑消费品价格，服务价格受国庆“性价比”出行模式带来的“量强价弱”
趋势影响，价格中枢有所回落。环比来看，10 月 CPI 环比持平，价格持平上
月表现，其中食品价格环比由上升 0.4%转为下降 1.0%，主要受应季蔬果供给
充足影响。非食品价格环比上升 0.2%，涨幅持平上月表现。10 月核心 CPI 同
比上升 0.8%，较上月回落 0.2 个百分点；核心 CPI 环比持平，价格指数连续
两月保持不变。居民消费价格保持温和抬升。后续看，食品方面，蔬菜或延续
供给端结构性过剩，猪价拐点条件或于明年二季度末趋于成熟，短期反弹动力
不足，食品价格或延续下行。非食品方面，国际油价回落将削弱汽油价格对
CPI 的支撑，节后出行需求回落，机票、酒店等服务价格也可能随之走弱。整
体来看，食品价格下行压力延续，非食品价格支撑减弱，CPI同比或继续处于
低位。  
 食品价格同比降幅扩大，供给充足拖累菜价反季节下行。同比来看，受气候条
件适宜、市场供应充足影响，食品价格同比下降 2.9%，降幅扩大 1.2 个百分
点，其中鲜菜是主要拖累项。 食品细项中， 鲜菜、 鲜果价格同比分别下降16.9%、

2
3.8%，降幅均较上月扩大。鲜菜价格同比去年同期回落9.6 个百分点，同比近
十年季节性水平回落了 21.7 个百分点。鲜菜价格大幅下行，除秋季集中上市
外，前期受高温和台风影响的叶菜生产已充分恢复，本地蔬菜供应增加、 远途
菜成本下降，共同推动鲜菜价格反季节性下行。猪价同比下降 8.6%，降幅较
上月收窄 1.4 个百分点。农业农村部公布数据显示，10 月猪肉均价同比下降
11.16%，降幅连续 5 个月收窄。截至 9 月底，能繁母猪存栏数为 3602万头，
较 6月末下降 178万头，去化速度较快，相当于 3750万头正常保有量的 96.0%。
这一位置的政策含义是产能已从“偏多”转为“基本适配”，不再触发强制性
的产能调减干预。但猪价同比近十年季节性水平低了 15.4 个百分点，仍处于
较大跌幅区间，说明从供给收缩传导到出栏减少仍有时滞。牛肉、 羊肉同比分
别上升 8.7%、5.8%，相比去年同期和季节性水平均明显提升，反映居民动物
蛋白消费偏好发生变化。环比来看，食品价格环比由上升0.4%转为下降 1.0%。

食品细项中， 鲜菜价格环比由上月上升 0.5%转为下降 5.8%，蛋类价格环比由
上月上升 2.0%转为下降 5.2%，两者均低于去年同期和季节性水平。黄淮海、
长江流域蔬菜 10 月同步进入集中采收期， 供给集中放量带动菜价下行； 蛋价
回落则源于国庆假期过后需求回落， 叠加蛋鸡产蛋水平抬升。猪价环比由上月
上升 1.2%转为下降 1.0%，主要受国庆备货需求退潮影响。从最新的高频数据
走势看，截至 2026年 11月 8 日，同比方面，11 月初 28 种重点监测蔬菜、7
种重点监测水果和猪肉价格分别下降29.7%、6.6%、16.1%，降幅较上月均扩
大，并且低于去年同期与近十年季节性水平。 鸡蛋价格同比上升29.3%，涨幅
较上月扩大。环比方面，28 种重点监测蔬菜和鸡蛋价格分别下降3.9%、0.8%，
降幅均较上月收窄。7 种重点监测水果价格环比上升0.5%， 而猪肉价格环比下
降 4.3%，降幅较上月小幅扩大。预计 11月食品 CPI 同比拖累或进一步加深，
但环比下行压力有望边际缓解。蔬菜方面，随着秋菜集中上市、 南北供给错峰

窗口收窄，供给相对充足或推动同比降幅进一步扩大；猪肉方面，前期高存栏
向出栏传导仍将支撑供给，叠加消费需求偏弱，价格或延续下行。
 能源和 AI产业景气支撑消费品价格， “性价比”假期出行压低服务价格中枢。
同比来看，10 月非食品价格同比上升1.5%，涨幅与上月持平，保持温和上涨
态势。 消费品、 服务价格同比分别上升0.8%、0.7%， 涨幅分别较上月回落0.2、
0.1 个百分点。从服务和消费品细项看，汽油价格涨幅扩大 5.8 个百分点至
22.4%，带动能源价格涨幅由上月的7.3%扩大至 9.9%。扣除能源的工业消费
品价格上涨 1.2%，涨幅比上月回落 0.4 个百分点。其中，数据存储设备、平
板电脑、 计算机和移动电话机价格分别上涨45.4%、29.3%、23.0%和 16.7%，
升级型消费和 AI 相关硬件需求持续支撑；黄金饰品价格上涨4.5%，涨幅比上
月回落 18.0 个百分点， 主要因近期金价回调抑制了金饰品消费需求。服务中，
出行服务价格上涨 1.5%，涨幅比上月回落 0.6 个百分点。中国饭店协会调研

数据显示，国庆假期住宿企业平均房价总体有所回落，平均降幅为8.7%；餐
饮企业客单价同比平均增幅为 1.1%。企业普遍以更具性价比的价格策略吸引
客流，压低了服务价格的同比中枢。环比来看，10月非食品价格环比上升 0.2%，
涨幅持平上月表现。消费品价格环比由上月上升转为下降 0.1%，主要受能源
价格及金价回落拖累。服务价格环比由上月下降转为上升 0.1%，主要受国庆
出游需求推动。从服务和消费品细项看，工业消费品中，受国际市场价格变动
影响，国内汽油价格上涨 4.2%，涨幅比上月回落 0.7 个百分点。黄金饰品价
格下降 5.9%，降幅比上月扩大 3.8 个百分点。服务中，国庆假期出行人数增
加，宾馆住宿、飞机票和旅行社收费分别上涨6.5%、3.3%和 2.4%。预计 11
月非食品 CPI同比或小幅回落， 环比或由平转负。 能源方面， 国际油价明显回
落，布伦特原油 11月初一度跌破 100美元，涨幅相比上月收窄，对 CPI的正
向拉动或减弱；金饰价格冲高回落，对非食品价格的支撑作用或进一步减弱；

服务方面，秋假或对部分出行服务价格形成结构性支撑， 但机票、酒店等整体
仍处淡季，价格上涨动力相对有限。
 输入因素叠加基数推高 PPI同比，但环比涨价动能有所减弱。从同比看，PPI

### Page 3

请务必阅读正文后的重要声明部分
3
同比涨幅扩大 0.7 个百分点至 5.7%，增速高于 wind一致预期（5.37%）。生
产资料同比涨幅扩大0.9 个百分点至 7.4%，生活资料同比下降0.1%，降幅与
上月持平。分行业看，煤炭、 石油相关的能源化工链条对PPI同比的拉动作用
显著。煤炭开采和洗选业同比涨幅扩大1.3 个百分点至 36.6%，在PPI 细项中
涨幅居首。石油和天然气开采业、石油煤炭及其他燃料加工业价格同比涨幅分
别扩大 10.5、8.3 个百分点至 35.0%和 29.9%。化学原料及化学制品制造业、
化学纤维制造业同比涨幅分别扩大至16.4%和 16.0%。有色金属冶炼及压延加
工业、 有色金属矿采选业同比分别上涨 18.6%、16.3%， 但涨幅较上月均收窄。
计算机通信和其他电子设备制造业同比上涨5.9%，涨幅较上月扩大 0.1 个百
分点，显示 AI 投资热潮对价格的拉动逐渐向中游装备制造渗透。然而该链条
带动的行业范围较为有限，或不足以对冲能源退潮后PPI面临的回落压力。从

环比看，PPI环比涨幅收窄 0.5 个百分点至 0.7%，生产资料环比涨幅收窄 0.5
个百分点至 0.9%，生活资料环比由上升转为下降0.1%。PPI 环比的大部分上
行动力来自输入性地缘冲击和季节性能源价格， 涨价的扩散性较为有限。分行
业看，PPI上行动力高度集中于能源，但价格已呈现边际走弱的特征，与同比
高增的趋势形成背离。石油煤炭及其他燃料加工业价格、石油和天然气开采业
价格环比分别上升 6.1%、6.0%，环比涨幅分别边际回落2.3、4.4个百分点。
煤炭开采和洗选业环比涨幅收窄 6.8 个百分点至 2.7%。化学原料及化学制品
制造业、 化学纤维制造业环比分别上升3.1%、2.4%， 涨幅均边际加快。10 月，
PPI 同比涨幅（5.7%）与 CPI 同比涨幅（0.7%）之间的差距为 5 个百分点。
较 9 月扩大 0.9 个百分点，达到年内最大分化幅度。这说明企业利润或进一步
向上游行业集中， 中下游制造业及消费类企业则面临成本上升、 产品提价受限
的压力。预计 11月 PPI同比涨幅或逐步见顶，环比则可能转为负增长或接近

持平。能源方面，随着地缘风险溢价消退，国际油价持续回落，能源行业对
PPI 环比的拉动作用或有所减弱。然而安监政策趋严叠加冬季储煤需求增加，
煤价或保持相对韧性，对冲部分能源价格下跌的影响。
 风险提示：国内政策落地节奏不及预期，国际大宗商品价格波动超预期， 海外
地缘扰动风险。

分析师承诺  
本报告署名分析师具有中国证券业协会授予的证券投资咨询执业资格并注册为证券分析师，报告所采用的数据均
来自合法合规渠道，分析逻辑基于分析师的职业理解，通过合理判断得出结论，独立、客观地出具本报告。分析师承
诺不曾因，不因，也将不会因本报告中的具体推荐意见或观点而直接或间接获取任何形式的补偿。

投资评级说明  
报告中投资建议所涉及的评级分为公司评级和行业评级（另有说明的除外）。评级标准为报告发布日后 6 个月内
的相对市场表现，即：以报告发布日后 6 个月内公司股价（或行业指数）相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数的涨跌幅
作为基准。其中：A 股市场以沪深 300 指数为基准， 新三板市场以三板成指 （针对协议转让标的） 或三板做市指数 （针
对做市转让标的）为基准；香港市场以恒生指数为基准；美国市场以纳斯达克综合指数或标普 500 指数为基准。  
公司评级  
买入：未来 6 个月内，个股相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数涨幅在 20%以上  
持有：未来 6 个月内，个股相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数涨幅介于 10%与 20%之间 
中性：未来 6 个月内，个股相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数涨幅介于 -10%与 10%之间  
回避：未来 6 个月内，个股相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数涨幅介于 -20%与-10%之间  
卖出：未来 6 个月内，个股相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数涨幅在 -20%以下  
行业评级  
强于大市：未来 6 个月内，行业整体回报高于同期相关证券市场代表性指数 5%以上 
跟随大市：未来 6 个月内，行业整体回报介于同期相关证券市场代表性指数 -5%与 5%之间 
弱于大市：未来 6 个月内，行业整体回报低于同期相关证券市场代表性指数 -5%以下

### Page 3

Cathay’s View市場策略思維
市場焦點
1. 市場焦點：
• AI資料中心疑慮衝擊科技股: 民主黨在期中選舉獲勝, 多位民主黨州長重提AI建設禁令。市場擔心將影
響AI基建擴張, 參議員重提「超級AI禁令」更引發市場恐慌。
• AI熱潮面臨獲利考驗: AI算力需求仍強, 然市場進一步放大檢視「AI投資能創造多少收益」。AI基礎建
設受阻打擊AI供應鏈, 但資料中心回報率成長仍有利產業前景。
• 就業數據與美國經濟: 美國10月ISM製造業指數升至55.5創近4年新高, ADP新增就業8.6萬人, 優於市
場預期。經濟表現強勁, 通膨仍具黏性, Fed下一步仍傾向漸進升息。
2. 後續觀察重點：
• 10月CPI(11/10)、PPI(11/13)、輝達財報(11/18)。
策略思維
1. 股市-類股走勢分化: 選後政策預期變化, 加劇類股分化, 可提高非科技股比重, 透過持股多元分散, 降低投資
組合波動。科技股可逢低分批布局, 但宜控制持股比例。
2. 債市-Fed仍傾向升息, 殖利率高檔震盪: 經濟走強, 通膨具黏性, Fed仍有升息壓力, 資料中心建設受阻降低

資金及舉債需求, 10年債殖利率回落, 仍維持震盪格局。
3. 匯市-美元高檔震盪: 美元指數近期隨美債殖利率回落, 但受升息預期影響而維持高檔震盪格局。

Cathay’s View
民主黨在參眾兩院皆取得過半席次 美股年末表現
資料來源: 270towin.com, 截至2026/11/4。 註: 統計1986年以來資料。 資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/11/4。
美國｜期中選舉結果出爐, 民主黨在參眾兩院皆取得過半席次
• 美國府會衝突與「否決先生」: 川普失去國會支持, 剩餘任期出現跛腳危機, 目前白宮已研擬「無國會施政」劇本,
可能透過行政命令與關稅延續政策, 川普更宣稱將動用總統否決權來阻擋不認同的法案。
• 民主黨的制衡與調查: 民主黨宣稱將透過國會調查川普家族與政府機構, 更可能全面封殺川普的司法與高層人事任
命。金融市場並不擔心分裂國會帶來的政治僵局, 這將會削弱川普極端政策或激進財政擴張的可能性, 反而降低市
場不確定因素。金融市場更加關注的是, 選舉結果將主導科技、醫療、能源等核心產業政策的重分配。

Cathay’s View
美國AI資料中心禁令
資料來源: Bloomberg, 國泰世華銀行整理, 截至2026/11/6。
• 隨著美國期中選舉出爐, 民主黨多位勝選候選人已經在當選演說中, 再度將AI、資料中心及大型科技公司的監管問
題搬上檯面, 包括俄亥俄州、維吉尼亞州議員及伊利諾州州長, 他們強調從資料中心到AI安全, 都不能交由少數科
技巨頭決定。
• 整體而言, 川普政府加速發展AI的方向不變, 但地方政府對資料中心的土地、電力及環境審查可能更加嚴格, 根據
UBS先前預估, 2028年的資料中心擴建可能有30%受到衝擊, 高盛預估有45%的影響。
資料中心｜資料中心建置將受到一定的衝擊
州長／德州Gina
Hinojosa(政見)
1. 暫緩新建資料中心: 暫停新資料中心興建及核准, 直到州議會制定完善法規, 保障居民參與
地方開發決策的權利。
2. 取消企業補貼、落實成本自負：要求業者自行承擔電力、用水及電網建設成本, 取消大型
企業租稅優惠, 避免成本轉嫁給一般民眾。

州長／伊利諾州JB
Pritzker(感言) 強調伊利諾州已制定全美最嚴格的AI監管規範, 科技富豪不能凌駕法律。
州長／賓州Josh
Shapiro(感言) 強調不能讓大型科技公司制定影響未來的規則, 地方社區應有最終決定權。
參議員當選人／俄亥俄州
Sherrod Brown(感言) 「I do not work for data centers.」（我不是為資料中心業者工作的)。
參議員當選人／南卡羅來
納州Annie Andrews(感言)社區決策應由當地居民決定, 而非華府的億萬富豪或資料中心業者。
參議員／維吉尼亞州Mark
Warner(感言)
AI雖有創新與創造產業的機會, 但也有負面影響, 從資料中心到AI安全, 都不能交由少數科技
巨頭決定。

2028年的資本支出可能受波及
註: 5大CSP業者指谷歌、微軟、Meta、亞馬遜及甲骨文。
資料來源: Bloomberg預估, 截至2026/11/3。
• 在AI需求持續強勁之際 , 大型雲端服務供應商擴大資本支出已成市場共識 , 市場預估2026 年整體資本支出將達
8,000億美元, 2027年更有望正式突破1兆美元。然而, 隨著AI監管趨嚴, 加上部分州政府開始考慮暫緩資料中心興
建, 未來AI基礎建設的擴張速度可能受到影響。
• 短期而言, 預期2027年CSP資本支出仍具一定延續性, 但若地方政府陸續提高建置門檻, 2028年投資計畫可能面臨
較明顯的延遲或調整。後續應持續關注大型CSP的資料中心布局, 是否轉往政策較友善的州別, 或進一步擴大海外
投資, 這將是影響未來AI資本支出規模與成長速度的重要變數。

GPU出租價格仍持續上漲, 降低折舊風險 雲端營收持續高成長

• 隨資料中心積極擴張資本支出, 市場擔憂未來AI投資與折舊成本的顯著增加, 將高於未來營收增速, AI投資回報率

若由於AI資料中心基建受阻而影響算力供給, 資料中心的投資回報率提高, 大型CSP仍是最大受惠族群。

科技｜AI應用擴大, 軟體及終端收入明顯增加

步加速。隨AI投資逐步轉化為軟體訂閱、廣告及服務收入, 有助市場焦點由資本支出轉向變現能力。

儘管10月FOMC按兵不動, 官員態度仍偏鷹派 經濟數據支撐高利預期,  抵銷近期買盤效應
註: Logan為今年投票委員, Barkin為2027年投票委員。
資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/10/31。
註: 2-4月受就業與消費數據走弱影響、7-9月受就業及製造業數據降溫拖累, 經濟驚奇
指數回落; 惟油價與通膨預期影響, 美債殖利率維持高檔。
資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/11/2。
• Fed官員延續鷹派: 除10月支持升息的兩名官員以外, 近期多位官員亦重申持續升息方向, 成員涵蓋今年及明年具
投票權的委員。Fed態度暗示的漸進式升息立場大致與期貨市場預估一致, 料未來1年仍具2~3碼升息空間。
• 經濟數據強勁: 美國第三季GDP、10月ISM、9月JOLTS等數據均優於市場預期, 以及PCE增速持續走高, 經濟驚奇
指數的反彈, 恐緩和近期科技股賣壓 、高利吸引力推動的債市買盤效應 。雖今年經濟驚奇指數與 10年債利率走勢

時有背離, 但油價高檔、通膨預期擔憂仍使利率走勢不易呈現趨勢性下行。
• 展望後市, 1)期中選舉後的中東局勢仍有高度地緣不確定性, 2)季度再融資計畫反映公債供給壓力, 3)預估2027年
AI信用債淨發行規模 恐持續擴張。在通膨、債務供給與資金需求 三重因素支撐下, 高利率中樞環境將為市場常態。
債市｜聯準會官員延續鷹派、數據反映經濟韌性,  利率大幅下行空間受限

融資需求居高不下, 2027Q1升至8000億美元 新增發債需求更偏短天期
註: 採12個月滾動加總(12M rolling sum)淨發行, 近一年淨發行約2兆美元。
資料來源: Bloomberg, Sifma, 截至2026/9/30。
財政部｜美國2027年Q1借款需求攀升, 短債供給占比將逐步升高
註: 單位為億美元。
資料來源: 美國財政部預估。
• 最新季度借款預估顯示, Q3實際淨發債規模7420億美元, 大致符合8月預估值(7390億美元)。季末TGA餘額9840億
美元亦高於目標9500億美元, 導致Q4淨發債需求較前次預估下修100億美元; 惟考量期初現金餘額影響, 借款需求
實際較前次預估增加240億美元。2027年第一季借款規模預估8000億美元, 財政融資需求仍維持高檔。
• 財政部維持「Bills優先、Coupon穩定」的發債策略, 短期新增融資需求主要由國庫券吸收, 以避免中長天期公債
供給進一步增加。此舉有助控制發債成本, 但也意味市場對短端資金需求的依賴度持續提升; 目前Bills占未償還市

場性公債比重約22%(截至9/30), 高於TBAC(財政部借款諮詢委員會)建議的20%水準, 未來若借貸需求擴大, 市場
對流動性事件的尾端風險擔憂及再融資壓力的關注度將進一步提升。

Cathay’s View
第三季GDP年化季增3.4% 核心PCE年增率升高
資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026Q3。 資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/9。
• 美國第三季GDP年化季增3.4%, 增速創一年來新高, 並高於市場預期3.2%及Q2的2.2%。除了個人消費強勁, 民間
投資成長顯著: 在AI投資熱潮帶動下, 設備支出對GDP貢獻創2011年以來新高, 為支撐經濟成長強勁的兩大因素。
• 9月PCE與核心PCE分別年增3.6%及3.1%。以核心通膨觀察, 除了房租價格走高之外, 扣除住房的服務指標「超級
核心」亦同步反彈: 能源價格走升帶動運輸服務, 醫療保健、金融保險增速降幅有限, 核心通膨仍具黏性。
• 美國經濟活動持續擴張, 消費與企業投資表現同步穩健, 展現經濟韌性; 與此同時, 通膨增速居高不下, 均將支持聯
準會維持偏緊縮的貨幣政策立場, 隱含未來進一步升息空間。

10月ADP新增8.6萬與前月持平, 服務業仍為主要支撐 JOLTS反映就業信心改善
資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/10。 資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/9。
• 10月ADP新增就業8.6萬人, 優於市場預期(7.5萬人)。主要由服務業帶動 (貿易/運輸/公用事業+3.9萬、教育/醫療
+2.8萬、休閒餐旅+1.9萬), 但專業與商業服務(-2.7萬)及商品製造相關行業仍顯疲弱。
• 9月JOLTS職缺數+33.3萬至752.3萬, 職缺/失業比同步上升; 從新增比例觀察, 政府部門+10.4萬職缺(約1/3), 勞動
需求屬溫和回穩, 非私部門全面性爆發。細項方面, 自主離職數(轉職信心)反彈、裁員率減少, 均為正向就業訊號。
• 雖美國勞動市場自疫情後的高峰放緩, 但目前低裁員率 、穩定就業成長仍反映就業韌性。隨市場對勞動需求走弱
的擔憂淡化, Fed短期內亦無須過度關注就業下行風險, 政策焦點仍將著重在通膨進展。

英國物價與央行利率 MPC委員投票傾向
資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/11。 資料來源: Bloomberg, 國泰世華銀行整理, 截至2026/11/5。
英國BoE｜英央政策偏鷹調整, 市場上修升息次數
• 英國9月CPI年增率由8月3.1%升至3.5%, 能源與運輸價格仍是主要推手, 服務業通膨升至3.7%, 顯示物價壓力已不
再侷限於能源項目, 而有逐步擴散至內需部門的跡象。雖然失業率維持4.7%, 且職缺數持續下降, 但私人部門薪資
增速回升至3.0%, 10月綜合PMI升至53.1, 代表現行利率水準對需求抑制效果可能不如預期。
• 因此, 11月MPC投票結果由先前 6:3支持按兵不動 , 轉變為全面升息 。同步上修 2026、27年經濟成長至 1.6%和
1.3%。大幅上調通膨預期 , 預估將在2027年年中攀升至5%左右(不利情境下甚至可能升至 6.2%)。導致今年年底

升息機率提高至 50%附近, 2027年2月升息一次的機率幾乎底定 , 政策基調已由 「觀察能源衝擊是否短暫 」轉向
「預防通膨預期失控」。

英國公共部門淨借款占GDP比重預測 長天期利率
資料來源: OBR預估, 截至2026/10。 資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/11/3。
英國預算｜財政赤字上修與公債壓力仍在
• 英國最新秋季預算案延續 「財政紀律」主軸, 聚焦於降低家庭生活成本與支持企業投資 。主要措施包括向約2000
萬戶家庭提供一次性 £100能源補貼、將家用電力增值稅降至 0%的措施延長至2027年7月, 以及延長每公升5便士
的燃油稅減免與燃油稅凍結政策 。同時擴大服務業減免範圍 、提高酒吧與音樂場館商業稅減免 , 並將個人營業收
入免稅額由1000提高至3000英鎊, 銀行附加稅自3%提高至6%, 並加強稅務稽核以增加收入來源。

• 儘管新增支出相對克制, 英國財政前景仍較3月預算案略為惡化。預測顯示未來數年財政整頓速度放慢 , 對市場而
言, 這代表未來英國公債(Gilts)供給將維持高檔, 長端殖利率易保持在較高水準。

RBA調低成長率, 調高失業率與通膨預期 市場預期澳洲升息循環將更快見頂
澳洲｜澳洲央行如預期升息一碼, 但升息循環告終可能性大幅提高
• 繼9月按兵不動後 , 澳洲央行11月利率決議如市場預期升息一碼至 4.85%, 但同時出現3票反對, 同時聲明上刪除
「必要時進一步升息」的字眼, 市場解讀為「鷹派升息、鴿派暗示」, 本次升息循環可能接近終點。
• 總裁Bullock表示, 他們對成長、房市、勞動市場的下行風險權重高於通膨上行風險, 並主張考量已累積的升息與政
策時滯, 應等待更多資訊 。顯示RBA在打擊通膨的同時, 仍不得不向疲軟的經濟妥協 。考量升息循環已接近終點 ,
對澳幣的看法轉趨中立, 但對於澳債仍正向看待。

費半跌破7月低點, 健護指數衝高 美債殖利率回落, 地產指數反彈
資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/11/4。 資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/11/4。
美股｜選後政策預期變化, 加劇類股走勢分化
• 民主黨拿下參、眾兩院過半席次, 並在地方選舉大勝, 政策預期變化加劇美股類股分化。資料中心禁建風潮恐拉長
申請審核流程, 遞延AI伺服器訂單, 半導體股持續修正, 費半指數跌破7月低點; 相較之下, 市場預期川普政府激進的
醫療削減政策將受到制衡, 使得健護產業成為選後主要的政策受惠者, 資金明顯轉進。
• 另一方面, 若資料中心建設放緩, 對資金及發債的需求降低, 美債殖利率應聲回落, 緩解利率敏感型產業的評價壓力,
地產指數明顯反彈。市場資金由高估值科技股轉向政策及利率受惠族群, 短期類股輪動加快, 美股多元配置重要性
進一步提升。

AI需求強勁, 帶動AMD資料中心翻倍成長 AMD毛利率續揚, 顯示需求依舊緊俏
資料來源: Bloomberg預估, 截至2026/11/4。
• AMD第三季營收達133.8億美元, 年增45%, 高於市場預期, 非GAAP EPS達2.09美元, 年增74%, 同樣優於預期。
其中, 資料中心部門營收達85.5億美元, 年增97%, 創下歷史新高。此外, 毛利率達56%, 年增2個百分點, 營業利益
率則提升至29%, 反映AI業務規模擴大正帶動獲利能力改善。
• 展望方面, AMD對第四季及2027年營運前景展現高度信心。公司預估第四季營收可達170億美元, 年增約66%, 不
僅高於市場預期, 也意味營收成長動能將進一步加速。執行長蘇姿丰亦看好2027年營收成長可望超過80%, 主要來
自AI GPU出貨擴大, 以及伺服器CPU需求持續成長。不過, 遊戲業務營收年減40%, 顯示非AI市場仍存在壓力。

外資在韓股拋售力道仍大 韓股與韓元近期出現罕見的股匯反向
韓股｜近期外資拋售力道不減, 但韓股與韓元出現罕見股匯反向
• 外資對韓股的拋售未歇, 且此次企業回購結束, 本土法人與散戶買盤力道有限, 加上近期市場對於AI類股的疑慮加
深。基本面敘事改變與籌碼壓力下使韓股持續弱勢震盪。
• 另一方面, 韓股與韓元出現罕見的股匯反向, 在大漲/大跌的波動日尤其明顯, 與台股常見的外資賣、股市跌、台幣
貶的典型路徑相反。研判原因為(1)今年國際化改革後, 外資可直接於在岸市場交易韓元, 對本土匯市影響更直接。
(2)目前韓股外資的邊際流量以短線基金的快錢為主, 買/賣股同時搭配匯率避險/解除避險。整體而言, 韓股能否重
拾漲勢仍端看市場何時對AI產業的疑慮緩解。
資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/11/4。 資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/11/4。

Cathay’s View
五中全會要點 官方救市工具
中國｜著眼換屆, 五中全會先為未來一年定調; 人行等金融部門則聯手公布穩市政策
• 中共五中全會聚焦「持之以恒推進全面從嚴治黨」, 強調黨性修養、從嚴治吏、正風肅紀與反腐敗常態化, 並完成
重要人事調整與紀律確認。政治安全與權力集中置於優先位置, 這使得政策環境更偏向可控, 而非積極擴張。在考
核與問責壓力下, 官員避險心態可能延續, 進而影響經濟政策的執行力度與市場預期的修復速度。
• 與此同時, 人行會同國家金融監督管理總局、中國證監會三部門聯手開會, 重點有二: 1)將股票回購再貸款利率下
調一碼至1%, 以及2)第三批證券基金保險互換便利(Securities, Funds and Insurance companies Swap Facility,
SFISF)互換便利達1000億元申請(近前兩次合計), 以引導更多資金進入股市, 回應市場對「穩市」的期待, 有助提
振信心。
資料來源: Wind, 國泰世華銀行整理, 截至2026/11/3。 資料來源: Wind, 國泰世華銀行整理, 截至2026/11/3。

大盤企業獲利增長仍舊疲弱 A股指數與評價走勢
中國｜央媽祭出政策嘗試托底, 但大規模增量政策暫且無望, 加上國際氣氛影響, 指數需時間築底
• 在五中全會的政策指導下, 政治穩定先於經濟發展, 短期內難見大規模增量政策。考量現階段, 社會融資的年增速
仍偏緩慢, 顯示實體經濟融資需求與信用擴張動能尚未明顯回升。大盤企業整體獲利年增速依舊偏弱, 獲利修復基
礎不夠牢固。這進一步限制了市場上行動能, 加上近期國際股市氣氛不佳, 也使得估值修復難以一蹴而就。
• 考量數據與治理取向, 1)短線行情偏淡、指數需時間築底, 因為大規模刺激期待落空, 加上國際氣氛讓AI硬體股跟
跌, 長期均線呈現空頭排列下, 大盤轉為防禦與避險主導; 2)中長線在黨建先於發展的邏輯下, 資金還是只能流向國
企色彩高的類股, 加以人行將股票回購再貸款利率再度下調, 這應能為具有股息誘因的金融股形成支撐, 是大盤守

勢下, 非科技股的另一選項。
資料來源: Wind, 截至2026/11/3。 資料來源: Wind, 截至2026/11/3。

Bull/Bear: Globalwafers Co (6488.TWO)

### Page 6

Globalwafers Co (6488.TWO)
04 November 2026 Citi Research
6
Globalwafers Co
Company description
GWC is the world's third-largest silicon wafer manufacturer. The company
offers a one-stop shop for customers with its wide range of product portfolio
and global presence. GWC has 16 manufacturing sites in 10 countries spread
across Asia, Europe, and the Americas. Its product offering include annealed,
polished, non-polished, diffused, FZ, EPI, SOI wafers ranging from 4” to 12” in

diameter.
Investment strategy
We rate GWC as Buy as we expect its sales and earnings to recover in 2027.
We believe silicon wafer sector has hit the bottom in 2Q26, evidenced by UTR
improvements across 6” to 12” wafers. Although GM would likely face short-
term headwinds in 2H26 due to rising depreciation burden, a fire incident and
higher energy costs, we believe favorable pricing trend, full UTR and the
reception of US government’s subsidies would help offset the negative

impact, especially in 2027.
Valuation
Our target price of NT$920 is based on 28x 2H27-1H28E EPS, in line with
+1stdv of 1-year fwd PE in the past 5 years. We use this PE multiple to reflect a
gradually improving demand outlook for the overall semiconductor industry
from 2H26 onward, especially a more meaningful recovery in 2027 and likely
shortage. We believe the upcoming megatrend demand from AI, HPC, auto,
and Io T and supply tightness could justify GWC's valuations during recovery

backdrop from 1Q27 onwards.
Risks
Key downside risks that could cause the GWC shares to trade below our
target price include: 1) weaker-than-expected pull-in demand; 2)
undisciplined capacity expansion by other industry participants; 3) wafer
price decline driven by macro factors; and 4) late reception of the subsidies
from the US government.

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be

Earnings revision:  
We revise up our 2026 earnings estimates by 1% to factor in 3Q26 results (we revise 
down our 2026E revenue estimates by 18%). We revise up 2027/28E earnings estimates 
by 14%/23% to reflect a better product mix with higher contribution from the server 
business (we revise up our 2027/28E revenue estimates by 2/4%). We now expect the 
GM expansion to be faster due to the higher server business contribution, and thus 
revise up GPM by 5.3/3.1/4.4ppt in 2026/27/28E.

Valuation:  
We reiterate our Buy rating on Fositek with a new 12m TP of NT$2,390 (from NT$2,082), 
based on 22.6 2027E P/E (unchanged), which is the company’s average P/E since 2022, 
and factoring in our earnings revision.

### Page 3

Investment thesis and Valuation & Risks

Fositek is a global leading hinges supplier, mainly for foldable smartphones (56% of 
2025 revenues). We expect the company’s foldable smartphone hinges revenues to 
show solid growth in 2026/27E driven by (1) rising foldable phone market penetration 
rate; (2) expanding customer base, from China smartphones to more global-tier 
smartphones; and (3) stabilized smartphone market demand, encouraging brand makers 
to launch new form factors to attract consumers. Competitive edges of Fositek lie in its 
strong innovation (e.g., hinges for rollable phones, wearable phones), in-house molding 
and automated production to secure precision, long experience to support fast delivery, 
and leading customer base with expansion (e.g., supplying Huawei since the first 
foldable phone back in 2019).  We continue to see Fositek successfully entering the 
AI-related business, especially QD (quick disconnect) products for AI server liquid 
cooling. The company has the largest QD production capacity (4.5mn units/month) 
among Taiwanese thermal suppliers and will continue to expand if demand continues to 
accelerate. Furthermore, the company continues to make progress with its rail kit 
business, which will continue to break into AI servers (including both GPU servers and 
ASIC servers). Overall, we believe Fositek will successfully transition from a 
consumer-focused company to AI-focused company, with server-related business to 
contribute 94% of total revenue in 2028E (vs. 50% in 2025), and will see GM continue to 
expand to 37.2% in 2028E (vs. 26.3% in 2025).  
Price Target Risks and Methodology - Fositek 
Valuation methodology: Our 12m TP of NT$2,390 is based on a target 22.6x 2027 P/E, 
which is the company’s average P/E since 2022. We consider this multiple appropriate 
given the increasing contribution from AI-related products (including QD and rail kits) 
could offset the potential weakness from its foldable smartphone business. 
Key downside risks: (1) Slower-than-expected contribution from AI-related products, 
(2) slower-than-expected foldable phone penetration; and (3) Potential competition 
from more suppliers

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.
Coverage Universe: A list of all stocks in each coverage universe is available by primary analyst, stock and coverage universe at

https://www.gs.com/research/hedge.html.

E Ink Holdings Inc.  | Asia Pacific
3Q26 Missed on Margins
No strong near-term catalysts despite trough valuation.
3Q26  results:
• Off revenue of NT$9,936mn (-3% QoQ/-5% YoY), GM declined 6.5ppt QoQ
to 52.2%, significantly below MSe at 57.6%/consensus at 58.0%. We think
this is probably due to less-favorable revenue mix.
• Operating profit of NT$2,643mn (OPM 26.6%) was 21% lower than MSe at
NT$3,330mn (OPM 33.4%)/19% off consensus at NT$3,281mn (OPM 33.6%).

• Net profit  at NT$2,652mn (EPS of NT$2.31) was 24% below MSe at NT
$3,491mn (EPS NT$3.03)/20% off consensus at NT$3,307mn (EPS NT$2.79).
October revenue was NT$3,747mn (+4% MoM/+74% YoY):
• 33% of both our 4Q26 revenue estimate at NT$11,459mn (+15% QoQ/+63%
YoY) and  consensus of NT$11,263mn (+13% QoQ/+61% YoY).
Other announcements (see more in Other announcements ):
•   E Ink announced spending of NT$2.78bn acquiring the land and factories in
Guanyin (Taiwan) from its related party Chunghwa Pulp  for its large-size

signage capacity.
• It  partners with Taiflex  for the latter to do CCL pilot production at E Ink's
facility, with potentially room for collaboration on mass production in the
future.
• It announced it would invest NT$187mn in the rights issuance from its
related party SinoPac FHC.
Our view:
• 4Q26 revenue will need to grow by 10% QoQ to put overall 2026 revenue at
10% YoY (low end of its guidance at 10-15% YoY), which implies Nov-26 and
Dec-26 could be down low single digits % MoM each. While it still looks

within reach, we suspect this is at least partially riding the tailwinds of more
module shipments, which might continue to suppress its gross margin.
• Although the  valuation is at a trough level of 11x 2027e P/E, we see no
strong catalysts in the near term except for the potential fund flows amid
the subdued AI appetite. That said, we remain a believer of longer-term e-
paper demand across various applications.
• 3Q26 earnings call is scheduled at 2:30pm HKT on November 12.

Morgan Stanley appreciates your support in
the 2027 Extel Asia Research Survey. Voting
opens 10 November 2026.

3Q26  results
• Off revenue of NT$9,936mn (-3% QoQ/-5% YoY), gross margin declined by 6.5ppt
QoQ to 52.2%, significantly below MSe at 57.6% and consensus at 58.0%. We think
this is probably owing to a less-favorable revenue mix.

higher than MSe at NT$2,416mn (opex ratio 24.2%) and consensus at NT$2,386mn
(opex ratio 24.4%).
• Operating profit of NT$2,643mn (OPM 26.6%) was 21% lower than MSe at NT

$3,330mn (OPM 33.4%) and 19% below consensus at NT$3,281mn (OPM 33.6%).
• With smaller non-operating income of NT$406mn, net profit came in at NT
$2,652mn (EPS of NT$2.31), 24% lower than MSe at NT$3,491mn (EPS of NT$3.03)
and 20% below consensus at NT$3,307mn (EPS of NT$2.79).

Valuation Methodology and Risks
E Ink Holdings Inc. (8069.TWO)
Base case, 12x 2027e P/E. We believe this valuation methodology best captures E Ink's earn-
ings growth momentum. We view our target multiple of 12x as fair, considering our estimated
17% earnings CAGR for 2025-28. The implied PEG of 0.7x is largely in line with its typical level
in 2022-25, when the stock was trading at an average P/E of 22x one-year forward consensus
EPS with a 37% earnings CAGR.

Risks to Upside
n Firmer-than-expected ESL orders
n Stronger demand for e-readers and e-notes
n Better macro conditions
n Milder competition
n Brisker inventory digestion
Risks to Downside
n Softer-than-expected ESL orders
n Slower demand for e-readers and e-notes
n Macro headwinds
n Intensified competition
n More protracted inventory digestion

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

研究員/莊佳蓉 EVA8894122@uni-psg.com

雖考量 VR 整體遞延加上中系手機客戶拉貨趨緩，研究部微幅下修 27
年預估，但仍然維持富世達買進投資建議，主係：(1)3Q26 伺服器營收
占比大幅提升至 81%，帶動毛利率達 38.0%，創歷史新高並優於預期；
(2)QD 月產能已擴充至 450 萬顆 （+50%） ， 且2027 年持續擴產 ；(3)2027
年受惠 VR 放量，加上CSP ASIC 專案陸續貢獻，營運成長性高；(4)目
前評價已回落至 17.7 倍，位於低檔。綜上所述，研究部下修 2027 年
營收至 295 億元 （原估316 億元） ，EPS 下修至 121.74 元 （原估124.48
元），給予 24 倍本益比，目標價下調至 2,920 元。

重點摘要： 
1. 產品組合大幅優化，3Q26毛利率躍升至38.0% 
3Q26營收45億元，季增20.4%，年增26.3%，符合預期。其中：
(1)3Q成長主要來自伺服器QD，季增將近8成，主因GB300持續
出貨，加上VR200小量出貨；(2)中系手機部分，受記憶體漲價
及缺貨影響，終端拉貨力道疲弱，單季營收季減51%；(3)滑軌
部分，因通用伺服器記憶體缺料影響，季減將近4成。整體而
言，因QD營收大幅提升，伺服器營收占比由2Q的55%大幅提升
至3Q的81%，產品組合轉佳，故毛利率達38.0%（原估37.0%），
季增4.0ppts，優於預期。營益率32.4%（原估32.4%） ，季增
4.5ppts，符合預期；EPS達18.11元（原估17.57元） ，優於預
期。

2. 4Q26營收微幅下修，惟毛利率續創新高 
展望4Q26，考量中系手機客戶雖為旺季， 但受缺料及漲價影響 ，
拉貨態度偏保守。在VR部分，根據供應鏈調查，若以L11機櫃
組裝端來看，4Q26~1Q27僅小量出貨，真正放量須至2Q27，時
程遞延。但若以零組件端看，液冷QD屬於偏L6端的產品，出貨
時程較早，4Q已開始出貨。但考量放量時程及手機影響，研究
部微幅下修4Q26營收至51億元 （原估53億元） ，季增12.7%，年
增27.6%。毛利率部分，考量伺服器占比可望維持在高檔，上
修毛利率至38.6%（原估37.3%） ，季增0.6ppts；EPS微幅下修
至20.42元（原估20.89元）。

### Page 2

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  2

3. NV、ASIC雙引擎並進 
基本面仍維持正向看待，QD成長動能明確：(1)NV VR 4Q逐漸
出貨，動能延續至 27年；下一代Ultra設計將導入更高流量
ZQD(單價翻倍)， 促進價值提升；(2)AWS T3已配合客戶時程出
貨，新世代平台水冷滲透率進一步提升；(3)其他ASIC專案陸
續進入量產，本次新增MSFT Maia QD，預估單櫃約576顆(32顆
*18層)，Google TPU、Meta也陸續出貨。產能方面，QD月產能
已擴充至450萬顆（+50%），2027年持續擴產，預期提升至650
萬顆，足以支撐後續訂單需求。

4. VR放量時程遞延，保守下修2027年營收 
然而 ，研究部仍考量以下因素 ， 保守微幅下修2027年營收預估 ：
(1)NV平台部分，考量VR延後至2Q27放量，恐連帶影響Rubin 
Ultra，且目前機櫃架構亦尚未定案，ZQD用量存在變數；(2)
中系手機客戶整體拉貨動能偏弱， 研究部預估明年衰退，故些
微下修27年預估。

5. 目前評價已回檔，潛在空間浮現，維持買進建議 
綜上所述 ， 研究部下修2026年營收至175億元 （原估178億元） ，
年增41.0%，惟毛利率上修至36.0%（原估35.4%），EPS上修至
64.30元 （原估64.20元）。同步下修2027年營收至295億元 （原
估316億元），EPS微幅下修至121.74元 （原估124.48元） 。目前
評價已回落至17.7倍，位於低檔，潛在空間已浮現，給予24倍
本益比，目標價由3,115元調整至2,920元，維持買進建議。

Units lift the headline
2 November 2026
Key points
• Total dwelling approvals rose 5.1%mth
in September, although the annual pace
moved into contraction at –0.8%yr with
the detail suggesting the underlying
cycle is starting to turn down.

• The headline increase was driven by
another volatile rise in private unit
approvals (+16.6%) which more than
offset a significant decline in private
detached house approvals (–5.3%).
• Units drove gains in NSW and
Queensland. WA, SA and Victoria all
recorded declines. Detached dwelling
approvals fell across all states.
• The total value of residential approvals
fell 2.8%mth but a sharp rise in non-
residential approvals – led by the
data centres – saw the total value of

building approvals rise 13%mth.
• Interest rate increases, weakness in
established housing markets, rising costs
and pessimistic consumer sentiment
are likely to remain headwinds.

### Page 2

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
Total dwelling approvals rose 5.1%mth in September, while
the annual pace moved into contraction at –0.8%yr. The
result was well above both Westpac’s expectation for a
7.0%mth decline and the market expectation for a 2.0%mth
rise. Unit approvals continued to drive headline volatility,

surging in September, while detached dwellings, which
provide a better read on the underlying trend, recorded a
sizeable decline.
Note that most figures in the charts and table are presented on
a rolling-three month basis to smooth out monthly volatility.
Private detached dwelling approvals fell 5.3%mth in
September, unwinding August’s upwardly-revised 5.4%mth
rise (+1.7ppts). Recent resilience has kept the segment
elevated, with approvals remaining above 10,000 and the

annual pace at 9.6%yr. However, the quarterly profile provides
a clearer read on the underlying trend and suggests detached
dwelling approvals are beginning to respond to recent interest
rate increases (see here). Approvals rose just 0.9%qtr in
September vs 3.1%qtr in June and 5.9%qtr in March.
Private unit approvals remained volatile, rising 16.6%mth in
September following 21.7%mth decline in August. Strength
was led by high-rise approvals, while low-mid-rise approvals

recorded a more modest increase. Despite the monthly rise,
the annual pace fell further into contraction at –20.5%yr.
Among the states, NSW recorded the strongest result,
with private approvals rising 35.3%mth led by a surge in
private unit approvals. Queensland also recorded an increase
(+9.3%mth), while WA (–2.9%mth), SA (–3.5%mth) and
Victoria (–29.0%mth) all declined.
In value terms, residential building approvals fell 2.8%mth,
driven by a 3.3%mth decline in new residential approvals,

partially offset by a 0.8%mth rise in renovation activity.
Non-residential approvals once again reversed sharply, rising
45.9%mth, as the data centre-dominated ‘other commercial’
segment recorded an 8-fold increase on a non-seasonally
adjusted basis. The total value of building approvals rose
13.0%mth in September. On a non-seasonally adjusted basis,
the value of building approvals rose 8.3%mth overall, but
declined 3.0%mth when excluding the ‘other commercial’

segment.
September’s result reinforces the importance of looking
through headline volatility in unit approvals. While total
approvals rose in the month, detached dwelling approvals
slowed on a quarterly basis and fell across all states, pointing
to softer underlying momentum. The annual pace of total
approvals also moved into contraction for the first time
since January. Interest rate increases, the sharp downturn
in established housing markets and rising costs are likely to

weigh on further growth, with pessimistic consumer sentiment
an additional headwind (see here).
Detached approvals point to softer momentum

2 of 8 November 9, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
Setting the Stage for 2027
Chief Investment Office, Macro Strategy Team
Typically, a new administration sets the stage in their first year with stimulative policy
changes that play out in the second year after the new policies have been enacted into
law and begin to have their impact. This pattern shows up in historical data with the
average rate of gross domestic product (GDP) growth highest in the second year of

presidential terms compared to the other three years of the presidential term. At the
same time, equity market returns tend to be lowest on average in the second year of
presidential terms, which seems to be at odds with the fact that growth is strongest.
This apparent inconsistency can be attributed to the fact that the stock market is a
leading indicator that anticipates stronger growth and higher profits well before they
actually arrive. Once they do, as they have in 2026, the markets are looking ahead to the

inevitable slowing from the policy-induced peak in growth that typically arrives after the
midterm election year. The midterms also create uncertainty about the subsequent policy
environment which damps stocks ahead of the election only to be reversed on average
once the elections are over and the outlook is clarified. This phenomenon is seen in the
fact that on average the stock market has corrected about 20% ahead of midterms only
to rise about 30% from that bottom in the year after.

Q3 GDP data illustrate this pattern. Economic growth picked up through the first three
quarters of 2026 to a peak of 3.4%. Consumer spending growth averaged 3.8% over the
past six months, while the AI investment boom accelerated at a midteens growth rate.
Private sector purchases were growing at just shy of a 5% growth rate. GDP growth is
lower than that because imports of Asia produced technology-gear for the AI buildout
are subtracting a good bit from that underlying 5% domestic demand growth pace.

The new policies aimed at helping consumers through lower taxes and investment
incentives to spur private sector production have boosted growth as expected. Still, this
acceleration is passing, and the fiscal boost is likely to fade in 2027. Profits naturally
have followed this stronger GDP growth higher.
The upward trend in analysts' earnings revisions has started to roll over in our view, and,
while earnings are still growing, the rate of growth is likely to slow from here. In simple

terms, the second derivative of growth has turned negative. From way above trend
toward trend growth is still sufficient for the bull market to continue.
Another aspect of this transition to midcycle aside from the fading midterm
election boost from fiscal measures is the shift of monetary policy from maximum
accommodation to a more neutral policy. The Fed's rate cuts helped the midterm
acceleration in growth. Its shift to removing accommodation with rate hikes this year

adds to the odds of some momentum loss in 2027. That's already apparent as Federal
Reserve (Fed) Chair Warsh noted in his press conference after the September Federal
Open Market Committee meeting. Weaker balance sheet borrowers have seen their
spreads blow out as credit becomes more expensive. So far, the damage is confined to
the lowest-quality borrowers. Before, these spreads were unusually low reflecting easy
money conditions that are now normalizing. It's still early days in the new credit cycle.

Of course, how the market responds to this new Fed tightening cycle depends on how
aggressively the Fed raises rates. Analysts at Ned Davis Research note the S&P 500
has gained an average of 4.4% in the first year of all postwar tightening cycles. This
outcome varies considerably depending on the extent of the tightening. Aggressive
tightening cycles have seen an average decline of 3.6% a year later, whereas gradual
tightenings have seen a 10.5% average gain. In short, as one might expect, too much

tightening can cause a decline in stocks. Currently, the market seems to anticipate a
Investment Implications
Equities should continue to outperform
bonds in 2027 in our view, but
performance is likely to shift in favor of
higher-quality, less leveraged segments
of the market as financial conditions
tighten and earnings momentum slows,
while the accelerating momentum of
the early cycle transitions to midcycle
cruise control.

### Page 3

3 of 8 November 9, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
December and March nudge up in rates to reverse the easings by the Powell Fed. This
would be consistent with an ongoing equity bull market in 2027. Obviously, inflation
will have a lot to say about whether a mild or aggressive tightening cycle is called for.
With wage pressures benign, productivity strong and the early-cycle acceleration set to
peak, we believe the odds of an aggressive tightening cycle that derails the bull market

are low.
Other policy effects have shaped the current economic cycle. The shift toward more
nationalistic trade policies with tariffs, for example, has drastically impacted the pattern
of trade flows. U.S. imports from China have dropped from about 20% 10 years ago to
less than 10%, according to research from Strategas. Nevertheless, contrary to concerns
of a trade-induced recession, overall trade has continued to grow in line with the global
economy. Rather than deglobalization, the world is undergoing a reglobalization into new

trade spheres that better align with national interests.
According to McKinsey Quarterly, "The 2025 U.S. National Security Strategy, for
example, reinforces economic and supply chain security, industrial capacity, technological
leadership, and capital market strength as national priorities. China's latest five-year plan,
meanwhile, includes technological self-reliance, economic stability and the modernization
of core industries among its key pillars. In this environment, value creation is increasingly

shaped by new trade corridors, growing defense spending, expansion of export controls
and industrial policies, and shifting flows of foreign investment." 1 These new trade
policies are an impetus for growth that is structural, helping to make for a strong
investment cycle.
Obviously, AI is a big spur for investment that has been facilitated by the new policies
such as accelerated depreciation of capital expenditures (capex). McKinsey Research
designates 18 arenas with cutting-edge industries they believe are driving the global

growth story for the next couple of decades. They note companies headquartered in
the U.S. and China account for about 90% of these arenas' market value today. U.S.
companies account for 75% of the market capitalization within the 18 arenas and
generate about 50% of revenues. Given its leading role in the arenas driving growth
into the future, we believe the U.S. is well poised to outperform as the world moves
into this new technological future. The 18 arenas are part of the economy growing by

several multiples of the old economy segments and help explain why U.S. Equities have a
persistent valuation premium over the rest of the world.
Because of the unusually rapid growth of these new technologies associated with the
singularity, while earnings growth may decelerate from its peak, there are structural
reasons to believe earnings are on a new, higher-growth trajectory and unlikely to revert
fully back to historical norms. The new technologies have much higher margins than the

old economy sectors. The combination of faster growth and higher margins bodes well
for the equity market as these new arenas of future economic growth become a bigger
part of the U.S. and global economies.
The midterms have changed views about the relative performance of some segments
of the economy. For example, strong sentiment to slow the buildout of AI data centers
was dramatically evident in the election results. Beneficiaries of the buildout are being

revalued to reflect this slower growth. Disapproval of the Iran War was also registered
in the results. Energy prices have dropped on hopes for a quicker end to the war. This in
turn has lowered inflation expectations and interest rates. Overall, while the midterms
have remixed relative performance between sectors they have not changed the outlook
for continued solid growth and an ongoing bull market in equities.
1 McKinsey Quarterly, Q3 2026, page 52.

### Page 4

4 of 8 November 9, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MARKET VIEW
China, the WTO and the Great Convergence No One Expected
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
Twenty-five years ago this December, the West made one of the biggest economic bets
of the modern era: It brought China into the WTO, convinced that membership would
transform the communist giant into a more open and market-oriented economy.
The logic seemed impeccable at the time. The Soviet Union had long been vanquished.

American capitalism appeared triumphant. Free trade, deregulation, privatization and
unfettered cross-border flows of goods, services, capital and labor were widely viewed as
essential ingredients of economic prosperity. Globalization ruled.
Fold China into the rules-based system, the thinking went, and economic liberalization
would gradually pull Beijing toward Western norms. China would become a willing
participant in the U.S.-led global economic order. The world would change China, in

other words.
Not quite. At the dawn of the 25th anniversary of China’s WTO accession, the great irony is
not how China became more like the West. It is how the West became more like China.
In the U.S., and across Europe and Asia, governments are increasingly embracing the
elements of China’s economic playbook, which range from state subsidies to the promotion
of national champions to strategic industrial policies, among other initiatives antithetical to

the global economic norms that prevailed when China joined the WTO.
The deployment of trade tariffs, the subsidization of semiconductor production, the
promotion of domestic manufacturing, and the screening of foreign investment are no
longer hallmarks of China alone. They are increasingly features of economic policymaking
in the U.S., the one-time champion of globalization. The European Union, Japan, South
Korea and other nations that once carried the flag for free trade and open markets have

moved much in the same direction. Even the World Bank—a chief architect and guardian of
Western-led globalization—has come around to blessing the merits of industrial policy, a
strategy central to China’s economic ascent.
To be sure, Washington has not embraced China’s economic model wholesale. Neither have
Brussels, Tokyo or Seoul. The political, economic and institutional differences between
these systems and China’s remain vast. But the direction—set by China—is unmistakable.

It was not supposed to be this way. To gain admission to the WTO, China made significant
concessions over 15 years of negotiations. Beijing slashed tariffs on industrial goods and
agricultural products. Various sectors—manufacturing, distribution, telecommunications,
banking and autos—were gradually opened to foreign firms. Commercial laws and regulations
were amended or written, including rules governing intellectual property rights. Nontariff
barriers like quotas, import licenses, dual pricing and opaque industry standards were either

eliminated or phased out. State-owned enterprises were reformed and restructured.
These concessions—and more—were taken as signs that China was serious about
becoming a global stakeholder. It stoked optimism among senior U.S. officials that China
was inevitably bound to converge and embrace Western economic norms. And, for a while,
the bet seemed to pay off.
As China lowered trade and investment barriers at the beginning of the century, U.S. goods

exports soared. American firms poured capital into the mainland, attracted by an enormous
labor pool, lower production costs and what would soon become one of the largest
consumer markets in the world.
China’s massive labor pool-cum-ballooning middle class paid off handsomely for Corporate
America. U.S. affiliate income earned in China rose from just $1.6 billion (B) in 2001 to
nearly $9B by 2010, making China one of the most profitable markets in the world for U.S.

goods and services. American consumers, meanwhile, enjoyed the benefits of low-cost
China imports. And debt-laden America benefited as China recycled its excess savings into
U.S. Treasurys, helping to grease the financial wheels of America.
Investment Implications
We expect China will continue to rely
on exports and investment to drive
growth amid weak consumption and
domestic demand. Manufacturing-
heavy economies like Germany
remain particularly exposed,

reinforcing our neutral stance toward
Europe in portfolios.

### Page 5

5 of 8 November 9, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
But just as WTO accession helped boost China’s imports, it also supercharged China’s
export machine. Rather than shrink, as some optimists had predicted, America’s
merchandise trade deficit with China more than doubled between 2001 ($83.2B) and 2005
($202.8B) and continued rising thereafter. As the decade wore on, U.S. manufacturing jobs
and wages came under intense pressure owing to expanding China imports and the shift

in U.S. production to lower-cost China. Entire supply chains migrated across the Pacific,
decimating many manufacturing communities across the U.S.
China began producing and exporting at a scale the world had never experienced. In the
span of just a decade, China’s share of world manufacturing output soared from 8.7%
in 2000 to 18.1% in 2010, when the nation eclipsed the U.S. as the world’s largest
manufacturer of goods. Four years later, China was outproducing all of Europe—and has

never looked back (Exhibit 1A).
Today, China sits at the center of global manufacturing and trade, competing not simply in
textiles, toys and other low-value products but also in more technology-driven industries
like electric vehicles, drones, machinery and clean energy. China now accounts for roughly
28% of global manufacturing, according to the World Bank, well ahead of second place
America (17%). Meanwhile, the nation’s share of world exports—just 4% in 2001—now

stands at 15%, roughly on par with the combined market share of second-place America
and Germany (Exhibit 1B). The world is so awash with China goods that the nation is on
course to post another annual merchandise trade surplus in excess of $1 trillion.
Not surprisingly, China’s grip on world trade has significantly dimmed the brimming
optimism that greeted the nation’s WTO entry a quarter-century ago. Today, the consensus
is that China’s trade surplus has been aided and abetted by nonmarket practices and

policies that include forced technology transfers, currency manipulations, state subsidies
to industry, restrictions on foreign investment in strategic sectors, intellectual property
infringements and resource protectionism. Rather than dismantle its state-led, nonmarket
and mercantilist economy, China has tacked in the other direction, notably under the
leadership of Xi Jinping.
For investors, the implications have been profound. We now live in a world where

governments increasingly decide which industries are strategically indispensable, which
technologies must remain domestically controlled and which companies require public
financing. The “Commanding Heights”—or who controls the economy—have shifted
from the private sector to the public sector. In this world, investment returns are not only
determined by traditional metrics like earnings, the cost of capital and valuations but also
by geopolitics, energy costs, trade protectionism and national security. The world today is

far different from the world when China joined the WTO.
Suffice it to say that the 25th anniversary of China’s entry into the WTO won’t be met
with much celebration in the West. The consensus in 2001 was that the world would
change China. Twenty-five years later, it’s China that has changed the world. It’s the great
convergence no one saw coming.

### Page 6

6 of 8 November 9, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THE TICKER TAPE
What are we reviewing to gauge the capital investment needed for the proposed Artificial
Intelligence buildout and the return required to cover its costs?
Christopher Hyzy, Chief Investment Officer
Capex by the 15 largest data center developers are expected to top $1.4 trillion in
2027, which is somewhere between 3.5% and 4% of U.S. GDP . A large portion of the
capital investment is earmarked for compute. The total capacity of gigawatts (GW)

under construction is around 34GW of Informaton Technology capacity, with the U.S.
accounting for approximately 70% of this, according to BloombergNEF. Given the
massive compute investment, we continue to analyze closely the total debt financing
announced, the approximate cost of capital of the capital providers, the associated
revenues needed to match or exceed cost estimates, and the individual credit spreads
(as well as credit default swaps spreads) of the larger hyperscalers and chip makers to

better assess the overall return on investment of the AI buildout.
BloombergNEF estimates, that at the beginning of this year, the highest-rated data
center bonds offered yields below 5%, which is now closer to around 7% today. In
addition, AI-tied firms have raised over $460B in debt financing this year, which is
more than double that from 2025. Finally, according to BloombergNEF and Bloomberg
Intelligence, as of September 18, 2026, it is estimated that the 15 largest publicly listed

data center companies would need to produce $2.5 trillion in revenues in 2032 in order
to achieve a return on invested capital equal to the company-level weighted average cost
of capital. This is about 5 times more than the expected revenue in 2026.
Recently, the concern across the capital markets is that there is not enough financing
capital to go around in order to fund the expected buildout, which is one of the primary
reasons why credit spreads have widened and yields have risen. We view this as a natural

development of such large-scale infrastructure transformations. This is the reason why
we continue to watch the overall health of balance sheets of the builders, the level of
profitability and margins, and the order backlogs/cancellations in the next few years. We
still believe we are closer to the middle of the AI buildout than to the end. Therefore, we
view larger-scale weakness in the higher-quality segments of the AI chain as potential
buying opportunities for investors who are underexposed to these areas.

oversight and data analytics. CIO viewpoints are developed for Bank of America Private Bank, a division of Bank of America, N.A., (“Bank of America”) and Merrill Lynch, Pierce, Fenner & Smith

The Global Wealth & Investment Management Investment Strategy Committee (“GWIM ISC”) is responsible for developing and coordinating recommendations for short-term and long-term
investment strategy and market views encompassing markets, economic indicators, asset classes and other market-related projections affecting GWIM.
BofA Global Research is research produced by BofA Securities, Inc. (“BofAS”) and/or one or more of its affiliates. BofAS is a registered broker-dealer, Member SIPC and wholly owned subsidiary

of Bank of America Corporation.
All recommendations must be considered in the context of an individual investor’s goals, time horizon, liquidity needs and risk tolerance. Not all recommendations will be in the best interest
of all investors.
Asset allocation, diversification and rebalancing do not ensure a profit or protect against loss in declining markets.
Investments have varying degrees of risk. Some of the risks involved with equity securities include the possibility that the value of the stocks may fluctuate in response to events specific to

the companies or markets, as well as economic, political or social events in the U.S. or abroad. Small cap and mid cap companies pose special risks, including possible illiquidity and greater price
volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.
Alternative investments are speculative and involve a high degree of risk.
Alternative investments are intended for qualified investors only. Alternative Investments such as derivatives, hedge funds, private credit, private equity funds, and funds of funds can result

in higher return potential but also higher loss potential. Changes in economic conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect your investments. Before you invest in alternative
investments, you should consider your overall financial situation, how much money you have to invest, your need for liquidity, and your tolerance for risk.

動態更新  運動用品  ‧美國

Under Armour 
(UAA.O/UAA US)

客流量下滑考驗 
重要訊息  
Under Armour  2Q FY27 (7-9 月；財年止於 3 月) 營運約當 損平。儘管 FY27
營收年減中個位數之 展望不變，管理層下調毛利率指引，反映考量油價、
運價和庫存清倉等衝擊迎面而來 。 
評論及分析  
2Q FY27 營運保持損平 。2Q FY27 每股盈餘 0.00 美元，符合預期。營收
12.4 億美元，年減幅度擴大至 7%，反映全產品 /通路需求 皆轉差。地區別
方面，北美、歐非中東及亞太平洋銷售分別年減 7%、9%、9%，僅拉美
為唯一成長市場 ，年增 3%。再加上通 路、地區組合不利與折扣力道加大，
毛利率延續衰退走勢， 年減 0.3、季減 7.1 個百分點至 47.0%，拖累營利率
至僅 1.2%，年減 0.1、季減 3.1 個百分點。  
產業困境 。管理層觀察到歐美客流量下滑與同業促銷力道加大風險，預計
3Q FY27 營收將年減 8-9% (連三季惡化 )，劣於市場共識之 4%衰退；其中，
北美及歐非中東將為 風暴中心， 分別年減低雙位數、高個位數，亞太則 將
改善至約年持平。加上油價和運價上漲、庫存清倉與匯率不利 等因素疊加
影響，FY27 毛利率指引下調至 47.2-47.2% (前次為 47.7-48.2%)，且年增
1.7-2.2 個百分點中約有 1.5 個百分點係來自美國退稅挹注 。 
庫存去化策略 。2Q FY27 庫存週轉天數年增 7%、但季減 17%至 149 天，
仍高於過去十年同期平均水準之 131 天，主因營運規模縮減凸顯在途存貨
較高。管理層表示 正與合作工廠協商推遲部分發貨，同時考慮 2H FY27 從
批發端去化庫存，但將避開同業清倉甩賣的場所。 公司計畫持續減少產品
SKU、聚焦高端或細分領域 (例如美式足球釘鞋已成為美國第二大品牌 )，
同時簡化工作流程 ，確保長期利潤率改善空間 。 
投資建議  
考量歐美客流量逆風 與促銷影響 仍延續，我們維持 Under Armour 「持有」
評等，目標價改採 FY28 每股淨值 1.6 倍推算，調整為 5.5 美元。 
投資風險  
上檔風險為消費較預期強勁與品牌 重拾人氣。

### Page 2

美國 Under Armour

十一月 09, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R  2

Under Armour 11 月 6 日法說會重點摘要  
 2Q FY27 (財年止於 3 月；7-9 月) 每股盈餘為 0.00 美元，符合預期。
營收 12.4 億美元，年減幅度擴大至 7%，反映全產品及全通路需求皆
轉差。受通路、地區組合不利與 折扣力道加大 影響，毛利率年減 0.3、
季減 7.1 個百分點至 47.0%，拖累營利率至僅 1.2%。 
 產品別營收：服飾  (佔總營收 71%)、鞋類 (19%) 及配件  (8%) 全面
惡化至年減 6%、11%、7%。 
 市場別營收：北美 (佔總營收 59%)、歐非中東  (23%) 及亞太平洋  
(13%) 分別年減 7%、9%、9%，符合公司指引。拉美  (4%) 年增
3%，為唯一成長市場。 
 通路別營收：批發業務  (佔總營收 58%) 年減 6%，再度轉弱，主因
全價商品銷售疲軟及部分分銷管道遇 逆風。直接面對消費者  (DTC，
40%) 衰退擴大至 8%，反映客流量壓力導致 直營店與電商業務分別
年減 7%、12%。 
 2Q FY27 庫存週轉天數年增 7%、但季減 17%至 149 天，仍高於過去
十年同期平均水準之 131 天，主因在途存貨較高 。公司正透過與合作
工廠協商推遲部分發貨， 同時考慮 2H FY27 從批發端去化庫存 ，但將
避開同業清倉甩賣 的場所。  
 FY27 營收維持年減中個位數之 展望，但基於油價和運價上漲、庫存
清倉與匯率不利等因素影響， 將毛利率指引下調至 47.2-47.2% (前次
為 47.7-48.2%)，且年增 1.7-2.2 個百分點中 約有 1.5 個百分點係 來自
美國退稅挹注。加上組織轉型與重組費用認列，預計每股虧損 0.02
至每股盈餘 0.02 美元。 
 短期而言，管理層觀察到歐美客流量下滑與同業促銷力道加大風險，
預計 3Q FY27 營收將年減 8-9%；其中，北美及歐非中東將分別年減
低雙位數、高個位數，亞太約呈年持平。 加上毛利率年增 0.3-0.5 個
百分點展望， 3Q FY27 營運落後市場預期。

圖 6: 公司概況  圖 7: 服飾貢獻營收最大比重 
成立於 1996 年， Under Armour 為國際領導運動品牌，致力
提供專業運動服飾、鞋類及配件；其產品採用創新材料技術，
包括吸濕排汗、溫度控管、緩衝與布料永續利用等，目的在
創造差異化，並提升消費者品牌 體驗。美洲為 FY26 營收主要
來源地區，佔比達 58%，其次分別為歐非中東 24%、亞太平
洋 14%、拉丁美洲 4%。

股價說明  等級 定義 
 增加持股  (OP) 對個股持正面看法，預期個股未來十二個月的表現超越凱基證券集團所追蹤的相關市場的 總
報酬。  
 持有  (N) 對個股持中性看法，預期個股未來十二個月的表現符合凱基證券集團所追蹤的相關市場的總
報酬。 
 降低持股  (U) 對個股持負面看法，預期個股未來十二個月的表現低於凱基證券集團所追蹤的相關市場的總
報酬。 
 未評等 (NR) 凱基證券未對該個股加以評等。  
 受法規限制  
未評等 (R) 
受凱基證券集團內部政策和 /或相關法令限制使凱基證券集團無法進行某些形式的資訊交
流，其中包括提供評等給投資人參考。  
  *總報酬 = (十二個月目標價 -現價)/現價

Democrats in the midterm elections. The key question is

decisive victory in the US midterms, claiming majorities in both the
Senate and House of Representatives. Though this outcome had
become the consensus in recent weeks, the scale of the Democratic
victory in the Senate in particular is noteworthy. Wins in Texas, but
also in less expected states like South Carolina, Kansas and Iowa,

Dealmaker-in-Chief or Mr Veto?
The Republican majorities in the House and Senate in the ﬁrst half
of Trump's presidential term allowed him to sign the One Big
Beautiful Bill Act into law in July 2025. Other legislation included
the Laken Riley Act, which tightened immigration and border policy,
the GENIUS Act, which created a regulatory structure for

stablecoins, the HALT Fentanyl Act on drugs and the TAKE IT DOWN
Act on online regulation.

### Page 3

The obvious outcome from the result on Tuesday is that a
Democrat-controlled Congress will stiﬂe President Trump’s domestic
agenda in his ﬁnal two years in power. A bill has to be approved by
both the House and the Senate before being passed on to the
president to sign or veto. With the Democrats having control of the
legislative agenda in the House and Senate, the president has
already warned that he will likely become “Mr Veto”. The only way
to overcome that would be for the Democrats to secure a two-thirds

supermajority in both houses. That isn't the case after last night's
results.
The consequence is that spending and tax proposals, as with most
domestic legislation, will likely be gridlocked. There are potential
areas of compromise tied to healthcare aﬀordability in exchange
for perhaps more spending on defence, but major tax and spending
changes appear unlikely.
On the one hand, this is often viewed positively, as it provides a
stable backdrop for corporate America. On the other, it means there

is little prospect of meaningful change to the nation’s ﬁscal stance.
While we won’t see any further ﬁscal expansion, large ﬁscal deﬁcits
and deteriorating debt dynamics will continue.
The ﬁscal situation will certainly remain a concern, although hitting
the debt ceiling could act as a temporary brake. The issue is likely
to come to a head by mid-summer 2027, with little scope for
compromise between the two sides in Congress. This risks an
extended government shutdown, potentially exceeding last

October’s shutdown, which depressed GDP growth in 4Q 2025 by a
full percentage point. The Federal Reserve risks “ﬂying blind” again
with no government economic statistics being collated or released.
What the Democrats can seek to achieve
Key sectors of corporate America will be nervous that Democratic
control of important oversight committees could bring greater
scrutiny. More congressional hearings involving AI and technology
business leaders would be a likely outcome, while a strong

performance at the ballot box may embolden local Democratic

### Page 4

leaders to try to stall unpopular data centre construction in their
constituencies. Fossil fuel producers may face similar challenges.
Democrats will likely feel even more conﬁdent about pushing back
against President Trump’s policies on Iran, support of Israel and any
escalation of tariﬀs. They may apply more pressure on the
president to sound tougher towards Russia and to provide support
to Ukraine, but the ﬁnal call will remain with the president.

Investigations into President Trump, his family and members of his
administration may also be stepped up as Democrats exercise their
greater powers. Inquiries into ﬁnances and business dealings could
result in subpoenas for documents and testimony. Moreover,
impeachment proceedings begin in the House, raising the
possibility of a third attempt to impeach the president.
To be convicted and removed from oﬃce, 67 of the 100 senators
would need to vote in favour, which is unlikely. The Democrats could

still use the process to constrain the president, but doing so risks
further toxifying the political situation in the US. Rather than
pursuing such action against the president himself, Democrats
could instead launch investigations into members of his cabinet.
With Democrats now in control of the Senate, they can block or, at
the very least, stall judicial and executive nominees. This includes
heads of government agencies, ambassadors and cabinet

members, potentially making it diﬃcult for the president to replace
any senior oﬃcials he chooses to ﬁre.
What will the president do?
A key question is whether a constrained President Trump, entering
the ﬁnal phase of his tenure, becomes more willing to accept
political or market disruption in pursuit of his objectives.
If there is little sense of any compromise, the ensuing gridlock in
Congress would leave the president to focus on areas where he can

exercise executive power: 1) foreign policy and diplomacy, including
trade policy, 2) national security and defence, 3) law enforcement
and regulatory policy, 4) personnel appointments (subject to
Senate approval), and 5) administration and management policy.

This suggests Iran, Russia/Ukraine and tariﬀs/trade policy are likely
to be the focus for the president. Tariﬀ policy could increasingly be
used to incentivise foreign partners to align with US foreign policy
objectives, for example by pressuring NATO members to increase
defence spending.
More fundamentally, heavy defeats in the House and Senate will be
interpreted as a referendum on President Trump’s policies and
potentially prompt ﬁgures within the Republican Party to take the

view that it is time to open the debate on its future direction. With
Trump’s inﬂuence set to fade, leading lights within the party, most
notably JD Vance and Marco Rubio, may attempt to shore up
support for their own campaigns as they look to the 2028
presidential election. Similarly, a large Democratic victory will
intensify speculation over whether the party’s 2028 candidate
should come from its more moderate wing or whether the
progressive left has enough momentum to put forward a candidate

of its own.
The market reaction
The initial bond market reaction has been a fall in yields across the
curve. In the coming days, that could reverse as the prospect of
policy stasis leaves the market weighing a still-elevated ﬁscal
deﬁcit against some easing of inﬂation concerns. We will probably
see front-end yields drift lower, while the back end retains an upside
bias. If we do see more tariﬀs, the back end could ﬁnd a small silver
lining in the additional tax revenues. But any beneﬁt is likely to be

marginal, given the risk that tariﬀs also push inﬂation higher.
Overall, we retain a bias towards the 10-year yield heading towards
5.25%.
The dollar is softer on the outcome, though it has recovered from its
lows seen in early Asia when a sharp fall in Nasdaq futures was
warning of a larger re-pricing of the tech story. Nasdaq futures have
since retraced the majority of those losses. Limiting a larger dollar
correction on this news is probably a still-resilient US economy and

at least one more rate hike from the Federal Reserve. The dollar
could see a little more weakness into 2027, however, if an

emboldened Democratic Party takes greater issue with Republican

What it means for Europe and China
With the outcome of the vote limiting President Trump’s options at
home, he is likely to focus more heavily on foreign and trade policy.
The result could be greater volatility and uncertainty for the global

economy.
This will clearly aﬀect Europe. A forced deal between Russia and
Ukraine, to the disadvantage of Ukraine and Europe, could
undermine economic sentiment in Europe. A new escalation in the
Middle East would push up energy prices and harm the European
economy. Europe’s latest shift towards more protectionist trade
policies could bring new global trade tensions.
At the same time, with only two years left in oﬃce, the TACO
(Trump Always Chickens Out) phenomenon could increasingly

shape Europe’s thinking. The risk of an unwarranted economic
accident rises as Trump’s policies towards Europe become more
aggressive and Europe’s own stance hardens, in the hope that
Trump will ultimately back down or simply run out of time.
If Republicans lay the blame for the poor result at the president’s
door, the sense that Trump’s time is coming to an end could grow
stronger. If so, some European countries may be tempted to test
the president’s patience and slow the pace of defence spending

increases, particularly given their own ﬁscal constraints.
Alternatively, and less likely, a stiﬂed but less angry Trump
administration could try to bring the wars in Iran and Ukraine to an
end. Constrained by Congress and the Supreme Court on trade, it
could ultimately, intentionally or otherwise, end up indirectly
supporting the European economy.
China’s main response will be to wait and see, as it will react to
actual policy changes rather than the shifts within Congress. The

Document 325: Financial conditions in the Eurozone_ a tightening temporarily tempered by credit volumes (2026-10-07)

Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-07
Pages: 3
Status: ok

### Page 1

7 October 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
FINANCIAL CONDITIONS IN THE EUROZONE: A TIGHTENING TEMPORARILY TEMPERED BY CREDIT VOLUMES
Thomas Humblot
*LATEST OBSERVATION: 5 OCTOBER 2026  **ESTIMATE
Since June 2026, the ECB has been publishing 1 a new indicator that helps to assess the transmission of its monetary policy to the economy:
the Broad Intermediation Gauge 2 (ECB-BIG). This new tool has revealed that, thus far, strong credit growth has limited the restrictive impact

of tightening financial conditions caused by rising interest rates in the Eurozone. However, this offsetting effect is beginning to fade.
The ECB-BIG: a new indicator for a broader view of financial conditions
The ECB-BIG complements the range of indicators used by the European Central Bank (ECB) to assess the effective transmission of its monetary
policy. This new tool provides a comprehensive view of how restrictive (or expansive) financial conditions are in the Eurozone. It stands out

for its approach that focuses more specifically on intermediation conditions. Therefore, the ECB-BIG provides a more comprehensive view than
most other financial condition indicators of financial conditions. In fact, it covers variables relating to the volume of bank lending to households
and businesses (the ‘volume’ component). Traditionally, this type of indicator tends to focus more on market variables (primarily interest and
exchange rates), share prices and corporate spreads. In addition, the ‘price’ component of the ECB-BIG is extended to include the cost of bank

loans, which is another new feature.
The rise in interest rates was temporarily offset by increased lending
By aggregating the variables relating to banking and non-banking intermediation conditions, the ECB-BIG shows that rising interest rates
(negative price effect) were offset in particular by lending volumes, which have continued to rise in 2026 (positive volume effect). A more
‘traditional’ indicator of financial conditions, the Macro-Finance FCI (MF-FCI) — which is, incidentally, included in the ECB-BIG — reveals a

much more pronounced tightening over the same period (see the orange curve in the chart). However, this offsetting of the price effect by the
volume effect is now beginning to wane, a sign that monetary policy is being effectively transmitted to the economy. Financial intermediaries
are gradually passing on the rise in rates to their customers (see the dotted green lines, which represent the composite indicators of the cost
of new loans to households and businesses, combining interest rates for different maturities), while lending volumes are falling. As a result,

between July and August 2026, seasonally adjusted flows of loans to households and businesses almost halved.
1 P . Lane, 2026, The Outlook for the Euro Area Economy, Background slides for Reuters NEXT Europe, London, 16 June 2026
2 A. Allayioti et al., 2026, The ECB-BIG index: tracking credit conditions in the euro area, ECB Economic Bulletin, Issue 5/2026
SOURCE: ALLAYIOTI AND AL. (2026), ECB, BLETZINGER ET AL. (2026), BNP PARIBAS ESTIMATES

In the longer term, financial conditions are expected to tighten, including as measured by the ECB-BIG
Despite the very sharp widening of sovereign spreads between 28 September and 2 October, financial conditions as measured by the
MF-FCI did not tighten, with sovereign yields actually falling over this period (with the exception of the OAT and the BTP), for example.

Nevertheless, the high volatility of the bond market, particularly in the sovereign segment, is likely to continue to affect financial
conditions. Looking slightly further ahead, the ECB’s key interest rate rises — the one that occurred on 16 September and another that we
anticipate for December 2026 (both +25 bp) — are likely to result in a more pronounced tightening. With a time lag of a number of weeks,
financial conditions, as defined by the ECB-BIG, are also expected to tighten. The price effect is likely to lead to a negative volume effect

on this occasion.

Document 414: USA_ Pushing Back Fed Rate Hike on Lower Core PCE Inflation; Q2 GDP Revised Up to +2.2%; Trade Deficit Widens More Than Expected; Lowering Q3 GDP Tracking to +3.3%

Source PDF: all report pdf/USA_ Pushing Back Fed Rate Hike on Lower Core PCE Inflation; Q2 GDP Revised Up to +2.2%; Trade Deficit Widens More Than Expected; Lowering Q3 GDP Tracking to +3.3%.pdf
Report source: Goldman Sachs Publishing
Date: 2026-09-30
Pages: 8
Status: ok

### Page 2

Personal consumption (third estimate) +3.8% (qoq ar) for Q2 vs. GS +3.4%, median
forecast +3.4%, prior revised +0.7%
Personal income +0.2% for August vs. GS +0.5%, median forecast +0.5%, prior revised
+0.3%
Personal spending +0.9% for August vs. GS +0.8%, median forecast +0.9%, prior revised
+0.1%
Advance goods trade balance -$132.6bn for August vs. GS -$116.0bn, median forecast
